# External dogfood: more-itertools/more-itertools

Group 2 of P9.9 ([ADR-138](../adr/CUPEL_DECISION_138.md)): the second Python repository. Cupel at `8b6e07b` (branch `p9-dogfood-python-java`) for the first runs, `650a00e` for the check's rerun, and `3a0630e` for the final survey.

## Repository

- **URL:** https://github.com/more-itertools/more-itertools, commit `4aeb433` (2026-10-14), default branch `master`.
- **Lines:** 16,726 lines of Python in 7 files (9,173 in `tests/`; `more_itertools/more.py` 5,644, `recipes.py` 1,617, typed by the stubs `more.pyi` and `recipes.pyi` beside them).
- **License:** MIT.
- **Suite:** `python -m unittest` passes: 929 tests (761 test methods, the rest the modules' doctests, which the test modules' `load_tests` add), about 20 s a run (`tests.flaky`'s reruns: 19–26 s each), 250 s under coverage.py; CPython 3.12.10 from `make dev-setup`. Selection: one of the most depended-on pure-Python libraries, no services, no build.

## Commands

From Cupel's worktree, with `C=target/release/cupel` (the very first survey ran the debug build at `8e8bef5`; see below), `R=<scratchpad>/p9-group2c/repos/more-itertools`, each command through `scripts/heavy.sh`:

```
$C init --write --cwd $R                                                       # kept, one key added (below)
$C survey --depth standard --budget 30m --no-install --format json --cwd $R   # 1 800 s at 8b6e07b and at 3a0630e, exit 5 (budget)
$C check --depth deep --no-install --format json --cwd $R \
   --paths tests/test_more.py tests/test_recipes.py                            # 431.5 s at 8b6e07b, 70.4 s at 650a00e, exit 0
```

`cupel init` wrote `[lang.python] test = "python -m unittest"` and `coverage = "python -m coverage run -m unittest && python -m coverage lcov -o {out}"`; the one key added by hand is `mutation = { jobs = 2 }` (the brief's two slots). The first survey ran the debug build: tree-sitter's queries unoptimized made `static.dead-code` (731 s), `static.deps` (533 s), and `tests.quality` (449 s) eat the budget, and every check after them was skipped; ADR-138 §2 names the release build, which every later run used. Even there, the survey ends at its budget (exit 5): `tests.mutation` takes 1,607–1,657 s and ends `partial` with no finding, and `tests.coverage` is skipped for budget. unittest has no per-test map (ADR-147 §5), so every mutant runs the whole 20-second suite; at two slots the budget holds about 160 mutants of `more.py`'s thousands, and a partial production reports none of them. With no mutation score or coverage for any production symbol, the survey's protect-first targets are all test functions (the ranking defect group 1 recorded), so the deep check ran on `tests/test_more.py` and `tests/test_recipes.py`, the files of its top ten: it grades the test code deeply and mutates nothing (test files are not mutated). Raw envelopes (0.4–7 MB) stay in the agent's scratchpad.

## Survey

Final `survey` at `3a0630e`: `partial`, exit 5 (the budget left `tests.coverage` out and cut `tests.mutation`), 792 findings (338 fail, 223 warn, 231 info). The survey at `8b6e07b` reported 2,224; the difference is the 1,560 mypy rows and the 4 `test.logic` rows the fixes removed, less 132 mypy rows that reading the stubs adds. ## Triage

Every finding of the final check, every finding of the final survey outside the check's two files, and every finding an earlier run reported that a fix removed (verdict `false`, marked by the fix); rows marked "(survey)" come from the survey. Verification per ADR-138 §4: every `static.analyze` row matched one diagnostic of an independent `mypy --strict --config-file setup.cfg` run over the same files (336 of 336 for the check; for the survey, 1,766 of 1,766 with the stubbed modules included and 338 of 338 without them, which is how the false rows were found); every cyclomatic count and function length was recomputed with `ast` (a lambda's decisions counted with its enclosing function, as Cupel documents); every `static.crap` row was recomputed from an independent `coverage run -m unittest` (67 of 67, the `def` line excluded); the share of each long or sparse test's lines inside literals was computed with `ast` (0.6 or more is the "literal table" or "docstring" reading); every `tests.quality` row, the cognitive counts, the nesting row, and at least ten rows of every other kind were read by hand.

## False-positive rate per check

`false / (true + false)`, disputed rows apart.

| Check | True | False | Disputed | Rate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `gen.property` | 164 | 0 | 0 | 0.00 |
| `static.analyze` | 338 | 1560 | 0 | 0.82 |
| `static.clones` | 42 | 0 | 0 | 0.00 |
| `static.complexity` | 18 | 0 | 13 | 0.00 |
| `static.crap` | 67 | 0 | 0 | 0.00 |
| `tests.assertion-density` | 4 | 0 | 24 | 0.00 |
| `tests.quality` | 90 | 14 | 32 | 0.13 |

Every other check that ran (`perf.bench`, `perf.footprint`, `spec.contracts`, `static.dead-code`, `static.deps`, `tests.coverage` in the check, `tests.flaky`) reported nothing: no rate; `tests.mutation` produced nothing to grade (see Commands). The `static.analyze` and `tests.quality` rates are those of the first runs; after the fixes neither reports a false row.

## Check bugs fixed

- **mypy reads a module's stub, not its implementation** (`static.analyze`): 1,560 rows (`more.py`'s and `recipes.py`'s 280 `no-untyped-def`, and 1,114 `no-untyped-call` in the tests, each a call into the stubbed package read as a call to an untyped function). Fixture: `crates/cupel-fixtures/repos/static.analyze/python/rates.py` with `rates.pyi`; tests `analyze::each_language_reports_exactly_its_fixture_s_planted_results` and `tools::mypy_checks_a_module_with_a_stub_through_the_stub`. Commit `3a0630e` (and `1e5ff3c`, the lint).
- **A decision inside a lambda is not test logic** (`tests.quality`): 4 `test.logic` rows (`test_key_function`, `test_collatz_sequence`, `test_filter_map`, `test_monotonic`, each handing the code under test a lambda whose body is a conditional expression). Fixture: `crates/cupel-fixtures/repos/python/tests/test_ledger_case.py` (`test_a_lambda_chooses_each_entry`); test `python_decisions::decisions_inside_a_lambda_are_expression_decisions`. Commit `650a00e`.
- **A statement that holds an assertion is not setup** (`tests.quality`; the pyflakes fix): 5 `test.duplicate-setup` rows of the debug survey (`BucketTests.test_basic`, `SeekableTest.test_partial_reset`, `StrictlyNTests.test_too_short_default`, the two `ClassifyUniqueTests` pairs), whose third shared statement is an assertion. Commit `8b6e07b`.

Rerun: the final check at `650a00e` (531 findings) and the final survey at `3a0630e` (792) report every row of the earlier runs except the removed ones above; the 132 mypy rows the stubs add are triaged as true.

## What a maintainer would do first

Fix `IequalsTests.test_not_identical_but_equal`, which calls `assertTrue` with two lists and so cannot fail, and give `ConsumerTests.test_consumer` and `TestSerialize.test_serialize_generator_methods_locking` an assertion. Then simplify `_islice_helper` (cyclomatic 23) and `minmax` (17), the two most complex functions, and fold the 42 clone pairs and clusters of the tests into tables, as the rest of the suite already does. The 338 mypy rows are the tests run under `--strict` against the stubs; the repository runs `stubtest` and ruff, not mypy, so a maintainer would configure mypy before reading them.
